<div class='question question'>


$5000 + {?} + 7 = 5087$

-  $870$
-  $80$
-  $87$
-  $807$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the missing value $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
5000 + a + 7     &= 5087 \\\\
a                &= 5087 - 5000 - 7 \\\\
a                &= 80
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$80$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the missing value $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
5000 + a + 7     &= 5087 \\\\
a                &= 5087 - 5000 - 7 \\\\
a                &= 80
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

$3.7 \times  9 = 33.3 \quad$  Write down the value of $\quad 3.7 \times 90$

-  $333$
-  $3.33$
-  $33.3$
-  $93.7$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 3.7 \times 90 \\\\
&= 3.7 \times 9 \times 10 \\\\
&= (3.7 \times 9) \times 10 \\\\
&= 33.3 \times 10 \\\\
&= 333 
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>A</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$333$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
&= 3.7 \times 90 \\\\
&= 3.7 \times 9 \times 10 \\\\
&= (3.7 \times 9) \times 10 \\\\
&= 33.3 \times 10 \\\\
&= 333 
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>A</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$333$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->

<div class='question question'>

$2 + (3 \times 6 + 4) \div 2 = {?}$

-  $17$
-  $13$
-  none of these
-  $12$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: Using BIDMAS
,

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 2 + (3 \times 6 + 4) \div 2 \\\\
&= 2 + (18 + 4) \div 2 \\\\
&= 2 + 22 \div 2 \\\\
&= 2 + 11 \\\\
&= 13 
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$13$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_bidmas,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
&= 2 + (3 \times 6 + 4) \div 2 \\\\
&= 2 + (18 + 4) \div 2 \\\\
&= 2 + 22 \div 2 \\\\
&= 2 + 11 \\\\
&= 13 
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$13$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

Decrease $\pounds 44$ by $10\%$.

- $\pounds 39.60$
- $\pounds 4.40$
- $\pounds 48.40$
- $\pounds 34$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin {aligned}
&= \pounds 44 - 10 \% \ \text {of} \ \pounds 44 \\\\
&= 44 - \dfrac {10} {100} \times 44 \\\\
&= 44 - \dfrac {10 \times 44} {100} \\\\
&= 44 - \dfrac {440} {100} \\\\
&= 44 - 4.40 \\\\
&= \pounds 39.60 
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>A</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 39.60$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin {aligned}
&= \pounds 44 - 10 \% \ \text {of} \ \pounds 44 \\\\
&= 44 - \dfrac {10} {100} \times 44 \\\\
&= 44 - \dfrac {10 \times 44} {100} \\\\
&= 44 - \dfrac {440} {100} \\\\
&= 44 - 4.40 \\\\
&= \pounds 39.60 
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>A</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\pounds 39.60$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

-  $6$
-  $18$
-  $21$
-  $3$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
p - 9         &= 12 \\\\
p             &= 12 + 9 \\\\
p             &= 21
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>C</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$21$

p             &= 21
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>C</code></pre>

</div>

</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

The next two numbers in the pattern
$1, 1, 2, 3, 5,$ are...

-  $7 \And 11$
-  $8 \And 12$
-  $8 \And 13$
-  $3 \And 2$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR:  Let's find the relation between the consecutive terms of the sequence.


The next term is calculated by adding together the last two terms. Hence, the sequence would be:

$
\begin{matrix}
&   \footnotesize {\maroonC {(0 + 1)} }
&&  \footnotesize {\maroonC {(1 + 1)} }
&&  \footnotesize {\maroonC {(1 + 2)} }
&&  \footnotesize {\maroonC {(2 + 3)} }
&&  \footnotesize {\maroonC {(3 + 5)} }
&&  \footnotesize {\maroonC {(5 + 8)} }  \\
1,  &&  1, &&  2, &&  3, &&  5,  &&  \ldots, && \ldots
\end{matrix}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{First missing number}       &= 3 + 5 \\\\
                                  &= 8 \\\\
\text {Second missing number}     &= 5 + 8 \\\\
                                  &= 13
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>C</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$8, 13$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_sequence_find_difference_note
<emptyline>
The next term is calculated by adding together the last two terms. Hence, the sequence would be:
<emptyline>
$
\begin{matrix}
&   \footnotesize {\rtq_katex_color_info {(0 + 1)} }
&&  \footnotesize {\rtq_katex_color_info {(1 + 1)} }
&&  \footnotesize {\rtq_katex_color_info {(1 + 2)} }
&&  \footnotesize {\rtq_katex_color_info {(2 + 3)} }
&&  \footnotesize {\rtq_katex_color_info {(3 + 5)} }
&&  \footnotesize {\rtq_katex_color_info {(5 + 8)} }  \\
1,  &&  1, &&  2, &&  3, &&  5,  &&  \ldots, && \ldots
\end{matrix}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{First missing number}       &= 3 + 5 \\\\
                                  &= 8 \\\\
\text {Second missing number}     &= 5 + 8 \\\\
                                  &= 13
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>C</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

<div class='question question'>

Which set of numbers have a total of $14$ and a range of $3$?

-  $3,5,6$
-  $4,4,6$
-  $4,4,7$
-  $4,5,6$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


ABBR: $\text{Range} = \text{Highest number} - \text{Lowest number}$


| Numbers     |       Total                                |    Range                               |
|:-----------:|:-----------------------------------------: |:-----------:                           |
| $3, 5, 6$   | $\green{3 + 5 + 6 = 14}$  | $\green{6 - 3 = 3}$   |
| $4, 4, 6$   | $4 + 4 + 6 = 14$                           | $6 - 4 = 2$                            |
| $4, 4, 7$   | $4 + 4 + 7 = 15$                           | $7 - 4 = 3$                            |
| $4, 5, 6$   | $4 + 5 + 6 = 15$                           | $6 - 4 = 2$                            |

Answer $= 3, 5, 6$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$3, 6, 5$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_mean
<emptyline>
rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_range
<emptyline>
| Numbers     |       Total                                |    Range                               |
|:-----------:|:-----------------------------------------: |:-----------:                           |
| $3, 5, 6$   | $\rtq_katex_color_answer{3 + 5 + 6 = 14}$  | $\rtq_katex_color_answer{6 - 3 = 3}$   |
| $4, 4, 6$   | $4 + 4 + 6 = 14$                           | $6 - 4 = 2$                            |
| $4, 4, 7$   | $4 + 4 + 7 = 15$                           | $7 - 4 = 3$                            |
| $4, 5, 6$   | $4 + 5 + 6 = 15$                           | $6 - 4 = 2$                            |
<emptyline>
Answer $= 3, 5, 6$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$3, 6, 5$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

A bag contains $3$ red and $6$ green marbles. If you pick out a marble at random, what is the chance that it will be red?

-  $\dfrac{1}{3}$

-  $\dfrac{1}{2}$

-  $\dfrac{1}{9}$

-  $\dfrac{2}{3}$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Probability}  = \dfrac { \text{ Favourable outcomes} } { \text{Total outcomes} }$


$
\begin {aligned}
\text {P(red marble)}               &= \dfrac {3} {3 + 6} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {3} {9} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {3} {3 \times 3} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {\cancel {3}} {\cancel {3} \times 3} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {1} {3}
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>A</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac {1} {3}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_probability
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
\text {P(red marble)}               &= \dfrac {3} {3 + 6} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {3} {9} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {3} {3 \times 3} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {\cancel {3}} {\cancel {3} \times 3} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {1} {3}
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>A</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\dfrac {1} {3}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='papername'>
<p>alderley-edge-school-for-girls--11-plus--maths--2019--sample-paper-1:1:19</p>

<div class='rag'>

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

$4$ girls have an average height of $1.4 \ \text{m}$. A fifth girl of height $1.2 \ \text{m}$ joins them.
What is the average height of all five girls?

-  $2.6 \ \text{m}$
-  $1.3 \ \text{m}$
-  none of these
-  $1.36 \ \text{m}$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Total height of 4 girls}     &= 4 \times 1.4 \\\\
                                    &= 5.6 \ \text {m} \\\\
\text {Average height of 5 girls}   &= \dfrac {5.6 + 1.2} {5} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {6.8} {5} \\\\
                                    &= 1.36 \ \text {m}
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>D</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$1.36 \ \text {m}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_mean
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Total height of 4 girls}     &= 4 \times 1.4 \\\\
                                    &= 5.6 \ \text {m} \\\\
\text {Average height of 5 girls}   &= \dfrac {5.6 + 1.2} {5} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {6.8} {5} \\\\
                                    &= 1.36 \ \text {m}
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

tprobability

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

Issy tosses a **fair coin** four times. She gets Heads, Heads, Heads, Tails. If she tosses it a
fifth time, is she **more likely** to get Heads or Tails?

-  equally likely
-  Heads
-  Tails
-  not enough information

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Probability}  = \dfrac { \text{ Favourable outcomes} } { \text{Total outcomes} }$


$\text {P(Heads)} = \dfrac {1} {2}$

It is equally likely to get Heads or Tails fifth time.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>A</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$\text {Equally likely}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_probability
<emptyline>
$\text {P(Heads)} = \dfrac {1} {2}$
<emptyline>
It is equally likely to get Heads or Tails fifth time.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>A</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\text {Equally likely}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

In the calculation $4 * 3 * 2 * 1$ each $*$ must be replaced by either a $+$ or a $\times$ sign.
Maggie wants to get an answer of $9$. In what order should she replace the signs?

-  $+ \times +$
-  $+ + \times$
-  $\times \times +$
-  $\times + \times$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$4 \ \boxed{\green{+}} \ 3 \ \boxed{\green{+}} \ 2 \ \boxed{\green{\times}} \ 1$

Lets verify.

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 4 + 3 + 2 \times 1 \\\\
&= 4 + 3 + 2  \\\\
&= 9
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>B</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$+ + \times$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$4 \ \boxed{\rtq_katex_color_answer{+}} \ 3 \ \boxed{\rtq_katex_color_answer{+}} \ 2 \ \boxed{\rtq_katex_color_answer{\times}} \ 1$
<emptyline>
Lets verify.
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
&= 4 + 3 + 2 \times 1 \\\\
&= 4 + 3 + 2  \\\\
&= 9
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>B</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$+ + \times$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

Putting these lines $||$ round a number or calculation makes sure the answer is positive; for example both $|8|$ and
$|-8|$ would give the answer $8$. What is the answer to:

$10 - |4 - 20|$?

-  $4$
-  $26$
-  $-6$
-  $6$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 10 - | 4 - 20 | \\\\
&= 10 - | {-16} | \\\\
&= 10 - 16 \\\\
&= -6
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>C</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$-6$

</div>
</div>

<div class='workingcodeblock'>

\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>

</code></pre>

<div class='question question'>

Work out $1638 \div 7$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 1638 \div 7 \\\\
&= \dfrac {1638} {7} \\\\
&= \dfrac {7 \times 234} {7} \\\\
&= \dfrac {\cancel{7} \times 234} {\cancel{7}} \\\\
&= 234
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$234$

</div>
</div>

&= \dfrac {7 \times 234} {7} \\\\
&= \dfrac {\cancel{7} \times 234} {\cancel{7}} \\\\
&= 234
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

Find $40\%$ of $\pounds 620$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 40\% \text{ of } \pounds 620 \\\\
&= \dfrac{40}{100} \times 620 \\\\
&= \dfrac{40 \times 620}{100} \\\\
&= \dfrac{4\cancel{0} \times 62\cancel{0}}{1\cancel{0}\cancel{0}}  \\\\
&= 4 \times 62  \\\\
&= \pounds 248
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 248$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>

&= \pounds 248
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

\end{aligned}

&= 40
\end{aligned}

$16 \times 15 = 240$

$16 \times \boxed{\phantom{15}} = 480$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
16 \times 15 &= 240 \\\\
16 \times 15 \times 2 &= 240 \times 2 \\\\
16 \times 15 \times 2 &= 480 \\\\
16 \times \boxed{30}          &= 480   \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$30$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
16 \times 15 &= 240 \\\\
16 \times 15 \times 2 &= 240 \times 2 \\\\
16 \times 15 \times 2 &= 480 \\\\
16 \times \boxed{30}          &= 480   \\\\
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>

$46 \times 44 = 2024$

$46 \times 22 = \boxed{\phantom{2024}}$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 46 \times 22 \\\\
&= 46 \times 22 \times \dfrac{2}{2} \\\\
&= 46 \times \dfrac{22 \times 2}{2} \\\\
&= \dfrac{46 \times 44}{2} \\\\
&= \dfrac {2024}{2} \\\\
&= \dfrac {1012 \times 2} {2} \\\\
&= \dfrac {1012 \times \cancel{2}} {\cancel{2}} \\\\
&= 1012
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$1012$

</div>

&= \dfrac {2024}{2} \\\\
&= \dfrac {1012 \times 2} {2} \\\\
&= \dfrac {1012 \times \cancel{2}} {\cancel{2}} \\\\
&= 1012
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
600 \div 24              &= 25 \\\\
\dfrac{600}{24} \times 2 &= 25 \times 2 \\\\
\dfrac{600 \times 2}{24} &= 50 \\\\
\dfrac{600 \times 2}{12 \times 2} &= 50 \\\\
\dfrac{600 \times \cancel{2}}{12 \times \cancel{2}} &= 50 \\\\
\dfrac{600}{12}          &= 50 \\\\
600 \div \boxed{12}      &= 50 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>

$12$

\dfrac{600 \times 2}{12 \times 2} &= 50 \\\\
\dfrac{600 \times \cancel{2}}{12 \times \cancel{2}} &= 50 \\\\
\dfrac{600}{12}          &= 50 \\\\
600 \div \boxed{12}      &= 50 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>

<div class='question question'>

A box contains bags of crisps.

Each bag of crisps weighs $25 \ \text{grams}$.

Altogether, the bags of crisps inside weigh $1 \ \text{kg}$.

How many bags of crisps are inside the box?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of bags of crisps $= b$

ABBR: It is really important to use the right units in calculations and convert where required.


The weight of box is given in kg and weight of crisp bag is given in grams. Lets convert weight of box in grams.

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total weight of the bags}         &= 1 \ \text{kg} \\\\
                                        &= 1 \times 1000 \ \text{grams} \\\\
                                        &= 1000 \ \text{grams}
\end{aligned}
$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
b \times 25                 &= 1000 \\\\
b                           &= \dfrac {1000} {25} \\\\
b                           &= \dfrac {25 \times 40} {25} \\\\
b                           &= \dfrac {\cancel {25} \times 40} {\cancel {25}} \\\\
b                           &= 40
\end{aligned}
$

Number of bags of crisps inside the box $= 40$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$40$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number of bags of crisps $= b$
<emptyline>
rtq_abbr_md_unit_note
<emptyline>
The weight of box is given in kg and weight of crisp bag is given in grams. Lets convert weight of box in grams.
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total weight of the bags}         &= 1 \ \text{kg} \\\\
                                        &= 1 \times 1000 \ \text{grams} \\\\
                                        &= 1000 \ \text{grams}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
b \times 25                 &= 1000 \\\\
b                           &= \dfrac {1000} {25} \\\\
b                           &= \dfrac {25 \times 40} {25} \\\\
b                           &= \dfrac {\cancel {25} \times 40} {\cancel {25}} \\\\
b                           &= 40
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Number of bags of crisps inside the box $= 40$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$40$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

A tin of paint can cover $45 \ \text{m}^2$ of wall. The school hall has $652 \ \text{m}^2$ of wall to paint.

How many tins of paint are needed?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Number of tins needed}  &= \dfrac {\text{Total area of the hall}} {\text{Area covered by a tin}} \\\\
                               &= \dfrac {652} {45} \\\\
                               &= 14.48 \\\\
                               &= 15
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$15$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Number of tins needed}  &= \dfrac {\text{Total area of the hall}} {\text{Area covered by a tin}} \\\\
                               &= \dfrac {652} {45} \\\\
                               &= 14.48 \\\\
                               &= 15
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$15$
</code></pre>
</div>

$

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

 A man travels $200 \ \text{miles}$ at an average speed of $60 \ \text{miles per hour}$.
 How long does the journey take in hours and minutes?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text {Time} = \dfrac {\text{Distance}} {\text{Speed}}$


$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Time taken}  &= \dfrac {200} {60} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {20 \times 10} {20 \times 3} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {\cancel {20} \times 10} {\cancel {20} \times 3} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {10} {3} \\\\
                    &= 3 \dfrac {1} {3} \ \text {hours} \\\\
                    &= 3 \ \text {hours} + 60 \times \dfrac {1} {3} \ \text {minutes} \\\\
                    &= 3 \ \text {hours} \ 20 \ \text {minutes}
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$3 \ \text {hours} \ 20 \ \text {minutes}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_formula_distance_time
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Time taken}  &= \dfrac {200} {60} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {20 \times 10} {20 \times 3} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {\cancel {20} \times 10} {\cancel {20} \times 3} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {10} {3} \\\\
                    &= 3 \dfrac {1} {3} \ \text {hours} \\\\
                    &= 3 \ \text {hours} + 60 \times \dfrac {1} {3} \ \text {minutes} \\\\
                    &= 3 \ \text {hours} \ 20 \ \text {minutes}
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$3 \ \text {hours} \ 20 \ \text {minutes}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

I have $30$ tiles to make a pattern on a grid. The pattern is a series of squares.

![alderley-edge-school-for-girls--11-plus--maths--2019--sample-paper-1/section-2-question-17-subquestion-4-00.png](/assets/alderley-edge-school-for-girls--11-plus--maths--2019--sample-paper-1/section-2-question-17-subquestion-4-00.png "alderley-edge-school-for-girls--11-plus--maths--2019--sample-paper-1/section-2-question-17-subquestion-4-00.png")

I have used some of the $30$ tiles to make my pattern.
Do I have enough tiles to make the next square, of side length $4?$
Show working to explain your answer.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Tiles needed}        &= 4 \times 4 \\\\
                            &= 16 \\\\
\text {Tiles used}          &= 1^2 + 2^2 + 3^2 \\\\
                            &= 1 + 4 + 9 \\\\
                            &= 14 \\\\
\text {Tiles Left}          &= 30 - 14 \\\\
                            &= 16
\end {aligned}
$

There are enough tiles to make the next square.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

Yes

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Tiles needed}        &= 4 \times 4 \\\\
                            &= 16 \\\\
\text {Tiles used}          &= 1^2 + 2^2 + 3^2 \\\\
                            &= 1 + 4 + 9 \\\\
                            &= 14 \\\\
\text {Tiles Left}          &= 30 - 14 \\\\
                            &= 16
\end {aligned}
$
<emptyline>
There are enough tiles to make the next square.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Yes
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

The **perimeter** of a regular hexagon is $42a + 18$.
Write an expression for the lengths of one of its sides.

![alderley-edge-school-for-girls--11-plus--maths--2019--sample-paper-1/section-2-question-18-subquestion-1-00.png](/assets/alderley-edge-school-for-girls--11-plus--maths--2019--sample-paper-1/section-2-question-18-subquestion-1-00.png "alderley-edge-school-for-girls--11-plus--maths--2019--sample-paper-1/section-2-question-18-subquestion-1-00.png")

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the length of one of its sides $= l$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
6 \times l                      &= 42a + 18 \\\\
l                               &= \dfrac {42a + 18} {6} \\\\
l                               &= \dfrac {42a} {6} + \dfrac {18} {6} \\\\
l                               &= \dfrac {6 \times 7a} {6} + \dfrac {6 \times 3} {6} \\\\
l                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 6 \times 7a} {\cancel 6} + \dfrac {\cancel 6 \times 3} {\cancel 6} \\\\
l                               &= 7a + 3
\end{aligned}
$

Expression for the length of one of its sides $= 7a + 3$.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$7a + 3$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the length of one of its sides $= l$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
6 \times l                      &= 42a + 18 \\\\
l                               &= \dfrac {42a + 18} {6} \\\\
l                               &= \dfrac {42a} {6} + \dfrac {18} {6} \\\\
l                               &= \dfrac {6 \times 7a} {6} + \dfrac {6 \times 3} {6} \\\\
l                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 6 \times 7a} {\cancel 6} + \dfrac {\cancel 6 \times 3} {\cancel 6} \\\\
l                               &= 7a + 3
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Expression for the length of one of its sides $= 7a + 3$.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$7a + 3$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

The **perimeter** of a different regular polygon is $75b - 20$.
The length of one of its sides is $15b - 4$.
How many sides does the regular polygon have?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of sides $= s$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
s \times (15b - 4)              &= 75b - 20 \\\\
s                               &= \dfrac {75b - 20} {15b - 4} \\\\
s                               &= \dfrac {5 \times 15b - 5 \times 4} {15b - 4} \\\\
s                               &= \dfrac {5 \times (15b - 4)} {15b - 4} \\\\
s                               &= \dfrac {5 \times \cancel {(15b - 4)}} {\cancel {15b - 4}} \\\\
s                               &= 5
\end{aligned}
$

Number of sides of the polygon $= 5$.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$5$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number of sides $= s$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}

s                               &= \dfrac {5 \times (15b - 4)} {15b - 4} \\\\
s                               &= \dfrac {5 \times \cancel {(15b - 4)}} {\cancel {15b - 4}} \\\\
s                               &= 5
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Number of sides of the polygon $= 5$.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$5$
</code></pre>
</div>

ABBR: $\text{Sum of angles in a triangle} = 180 \degree$

r               &= 180 - 164 \\\\

\end {aligned}

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

A cube measuring $3 \ \text{cm} \times 3 \ \text{cm} \times 3 \ \text{cm}$ is made up of small cubes and has a
$1 \ \text{cm} \times 1 \ \text{cm}$ square hole pushed right through the centre of each face so that you can see straight through the cube from every side.

![alderley-edge-school-for-girls--11-plus--maths--2019--sample-paper-1/section-2-question-22-00.png](/assets/alderley-edge-school-for-girls--11-plus--maths--2019--sample-paper-1/section-2-question-22-00.png "alderley-edge-school-for-girls--11-plus--maths--2019--sample-paper-1/section-2-question-22-00.png")

How many small cubes are used to make this shape?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Volume of a cube} = (\text{Side}) ^ { 3 }$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Smaller cubes without removing}   &= \dfrac {\text{Volume of the big cube}} {\text{Volume of 1 small cube}} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {3 \times 3 \times 3} {1 \times 1 \times 1} \\\\
                                        &= 27 \\\\
\text{Cubes to remove}                  &= \text{Cube on each face} + \text{Cube at the center} \\\\
                                        &= 6 + 1 \\\\
                                        &= 7 \\\\
\text{Cubes needed}                     &= 27 - 7 \\\\
                                        &= 20
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$20$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_volume_cube
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Smaller cubes without removing}   &= \dfrac {\text{Volume of the big cube}} {\text{Volume of 1 small cube}} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {3 \times 3 \times 3} {1 \times 1 \times 1} \\\\
                                        &= 27 \\\\
\text{Cubes to remove}                  &= \text{Cube on each face} + \text{Cube at the center} \\\\
                                        &= 6 + 1 \\\\
                                        &= 7 \\\\
\text{Cubes needed}                     &= 27 - 7 \\\\
                                        &= 20
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$20$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

For one of the spinners, the probability of spinning grey is $\dfrac{3}{4}$.

Which spinner is this?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Probability}  = \dfrac { \text{ Favourable outcomes} } { \text{Total outcomes} }$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{P(spinning grey in A)}       &= \dfrac {2} {3} \\\\
\text{P(spinning grey in B)}       &= \dfrac {3} {4} \\\\
\text{P(spinning grey in C)}       &= \dfrac {3} {5} \\\\
\text{P(spinning grey in D)}       &= \dfrac {5} {8} \\\\
\text{P(spinning grey in E)}       &= \dfrac {2} {6} \\\\
                                   &= \dfrac {2} {3 \times 2} \\\\
                                   &= \dfrac {\cancel {2}} {3 \times \cancel {2}} \\\\
                                   &= \dfrac {1} {3}
\end{aligned}
$

Spinner B

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

Spinner B

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_probability
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{P(spinning grey in A)}       &= \dfrac {2} {3} \\\\
\text{P(spinning grey in B)}       &= \dfrac {3} {4} \\\\
\text{P(spinning grey in C)}       &= \dfrac {3} {5} \\\\
\text{P(spinning grey in D)}       &= \dfrac {5} {8} \\\\
\text{P(spinning grey in E)}       &= \dfrac {2} {6} \\\\
                                   &= \dfrac {2} {3 \times 2} \\\\
                                   &= \dfrac {\cancel {2}} {3 \times \cancel {2}} \\\\
                                   &= \dfrac {1} {3}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Spinner B
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Spinner B
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

For two of the spinners, the probability of spinning grey is more than $60\%$ but less than $70\%$.

Which two spinners are these?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{P(spinning grey in A)}       &= \dfrac {2} {3} \times 100 \\\\
                                   &= \dfrac {200} {3} \\\\
                                   &= 66.66 \% \\\\
\text{P(spinning grey in B)}       &= \dfrac {3} {4} \times 100 \\\\
                                   &= \dfrac {3 \times 100} {4} \\\\
                                   &= \dfrac {3 \times 25 \times 4} {4} \\\\
                                   &= \dfrac {3 \times 25 \times \cancel{4}} {\cancel{4}} \\\\
                                   &= 3 \times 25  \\\\
                                   &= 75 \% \\\\
\text{P(spinning grey in C)}       &= \dfrac {3} {5} \times 100 \\\\
                                   &= \dfrac {3 \times 100} {5} \\\\
                                   &= \dfrac {3 \times 20 \times 5} {5} \\\\
                                   &= \dfrac {3 \times 20 \times \cancel{5}} {\cancel{5}} \\\\
                                   &= 3 \times 20  \\\\
                                   &= 60 \% \\\\
\text{P(spinning grey in D)}       &= \dfrac {5} {8} \times 100 \\\\
                                   &= \dfrac {5 \times 100} {8} \\\\
                                   &= \dfrac {5 \times 25 \times 4} {4 \times 2} \\\\
                                   &= \dfrac {5 \times 25 \times \cancel{4}} {\cancel{4} \times 2} \\\\
                                   &= \dfrac {5 \times 25} {2} \\\\
                                   &= \dfrac {125} {2} \\\\
                                   &= 62.5 \% \\\\
\text{P(spinning grey in E)}       &= \dfrac {1} {3} \times 100 \\\\
                                   &= \dfrac {100} {3} \\\\
                                   &= 33.33 \%
\end{aligned}
$

Spinner A, Spinner D

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

Spinner A, Spinner D

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{P(spinning grey in A)}       &= \dfrac {2} {3} \times 100 \\\\
                                   &= \dfrac {200} {3} \\\\
                                   &= 66.66 \% \\\\
\text{P(spinning grey in B)}       &= \dfrac {3} {4} \times 100 \\\\
                                   &= \dfrac {3 \times 100} {4} \\\\
                                   &= \dfrac {3 \times 25 \times 4} {4} \\\\
                                   &= \dfrac {3 \times 25 \times \cancel{4}} {\cancel{4}} \\\\
                                   &= 3 \times 25  \\\\
                                   &= 75 \% \\\\
\text{P(spinning grey in C)}       &= \dfrac {3} {5} \times 100 \\\\
                                   &= \dfrac {3 \times 100} {5} \\\\
                                   &= \dfrac {3 \times 20 \times 5} {5} \\\\
                                   &= \dfrac {3 \times 20 \times \cancel{5}} {\cancel{5}} \\\\
                                   &= 3 \times 20  \\\\
                                   &= 60 \% \\\\
\text{P(spinning grey in D)}       &= \dfrac {5} {8} \times 100 \\\\
                                   &= \dfrac {5 \times 100} {8} \\\\
                                   &= \dfrac {5 \times 25 \times 4} {4 \times 2} \\\\
                                   &= \dfrac {5 \times 25 \times \cancel{4}} {\cancel{4} \times 2} \\\\
                                   &= \dfrac {5 \times 25} {2} \\\\
                                   &= \dfrac {125} {2} \\\\
                                   &= 62.5 \% \\\\
\text{P(spinning grey in E)}       &= \dfrac {1} {3} \times 100 \\\\
                                   &= \dfrac {100} {3} \\\\
                                   &= 33.33 \%
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Spinner A, Spinner D
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Spinner A, Spinner D
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
</ul>

She asked some students in year $9$ which types of film they liked best.

She used the results to draw this pie chart.

![alderley-edge-school-for-girls--11-plus--maths--2019--sample-paper-1/section-2-question-24-00.png](/assets/alderley-edge-school-for-girls--11-plus--maths--2019--sample-paper-1/section-2-question-24-00.png "alderley-edge-school-for-girls--11-plus--maths--2019--sample-paper-1/section-2-question-24-00.png")

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What fraction of students said “Comedy”?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin {aligned}
\text{Fraction}     &= \dfrac {90} {360} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {90} {4 \times 90} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {\cancel {90}} {4 \times \cancel {90}} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {1} {4}
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac {1} {4}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin {aligned}
\text{Fraction}     &= \dfrac {90} {360} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {90} {4 \times 90} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {\cancel {90}} {4 \times \cancel {90}} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {1} {4}
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\dfrac {1} {4}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

$20$ students said “Horror”.

Work out the total number of students that Betty asked.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the total number of students $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin {aligned}
\dfrac {120} {360} \times a     &= 20 \\\\
a                               &= \dfrac {20 \times 360} {120} \\\\
a                               &= \dfrac {20 \times 3 \times 120} {120} \\\\
a                               &= \dfrac {20 \times 3 \times \cancel {120}} {\cancel {120}} \\\\
a                               &= 20 \times 3 \\\\
a                               &= 60
\end {aligned}
$

Total number of students $= 60$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$60$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the total number of students $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
\dfrac {120} {360} \times a     &= 20 \\\\
a                               &= \dfrac {20 \times 360} {120} \\\\
a                               &= \dfrac {20 \times 3 \times 120} {120} \\\\
a                               &= \dfrac {20 \times 3 \times \cancel {120}} {\cancel {120}} \\\\
a                               &= 20 \times 3 \\\\
a                               &= 60
\end {aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Total number of students $= 60$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$60$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
